Casting a <code>byte</code> to <code>char</code> first <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Sign extension" data-def="Widening a signed number by copying its top (sign) bit into the new bits, so negative numbers stay negative. &lt;code&gt;(long) -1&lt;/code&gt; is all ones." data-book="Java Puzzlers">sign-extends</button> it to 16 bits set, <code>\uffff</code>.</p>
<p>Casting the <code>char</code> to <code>int</code> <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Zero extension" data-def="Widening by filling the new bits with zeros. Java does it when widening a &lt;code&gt;char&lt;/code&gt;, which is unsigned." data-book="Java Puzzlers">zero-extends</button>, because <code>char</code> is unsigned, giving 0x0000ffff, which is 65,535.</p>
<p>Whether a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Widening conversion" data-def="Converting to a type with more bits, like &lt;code&gt;int&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code&gt;long&lt;/code&gt;. The value is kept; integers are sign- or zero-extended." data-book="Java Puzzlers">widening</button> fills with ones or zeros depends on the source type being signed — an easy thing to forget in a chain of casts.</p>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">}</span></span></code></pre>42:T45b,<p>When a generic class is used as a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Raw type" data-def="A generic type used without type arguments, like &lt;code&gt;List&lt;/code&gt; instead of &lt;code&gt;List&amp;lt;String&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt;. Exists only for compatibility; loses all type safety." data-book="Effective Java">raw type</button>, every generic type in its members is <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Erasure" data-def="Generic type arguments are checked at compile time and then removed, so at run time a &lt;code&gt;List&amp;lt;String&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt; is just a &lt;code&gt;List&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Effective Java">erased</button> — not just the ones involving <code>V</code>.</p>
<p>So through a raw <code>Pair</code>, <code>stringList()</code> returns a raw <code>List</code>, whose elements are <code>Object</code>.</p>
<p>The for-each loop then can&#39;t assign them to <code>String</code>. Declaring <code>p</code> as <code>Pair&lt;?&gt;</code> or <code>Pair&lt;Object&gt;</code> keeps <code>List&lt;String&gt;</code>.</p>
43:T9c0,<pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light github-dark" style="--shiki-light:#24292e;--shiki-dark:#e1e4e8;--shiki-light-bg:#fff;--shiki-dark-bg:#24292e" tabindex="0"><code><span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">class</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> Pair</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">&#x3C;</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">V</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">> {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">  List&#x3C;</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">String</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">> </span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">stringList</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">() { </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">return</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> List.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">of</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(</span><span style="--shiki-light:#032F62;--shiki-dark:#9ECBFF">"a"</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">); }</span></span>

style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">() </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">+</span><span style="--shiki-light:#032F62;--shiki-dark:#9ECBFF"> " "</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> +</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> IntStream.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">range</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(</span><span style="--shiki-light:#005CC5;--shiki-dark:#79B8FF">1</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">, </span><span style="--shiki-light:#005CC5;--shiki-dark:#79B8FF">5</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">).</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">count</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">());</span></span></code></pre>4c:T46a,<p>The three-argument <code>Stream.iterate(seed, hasNext, next)</code> works like a <code>for</code> loop: start at 1, continue while <code>n &lt; 20</code>, step with <code>n * 3</code>.</p>
<p>It produces 1, 3 and 9; the next value, 27, fails the condition, so the stream ends.</p>
<p>The two-argument version has no condition and is infinite, so it needs a <code>limit</code> or a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Short-circuiting" data-def="Stopping as soon as the answer is known — &lt;code&gt;findFirst&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;anyMatch&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;limit&lt;/code&gt; don&amp;#39;t process the rest of the stream." data-book="Modern Java in Action">short-circuiting</button> <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Terminal operation" data-def="A stream operation, like &lt;code&gt;collect&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;forEach&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;findFirst&lt;/code&gt;, that produces a result and runs the pipeline. The stream can&amp;#39;t be used again afterwards." data-book="Modern Java in Action">terminal operation</button>.</p>

data-def="An anonymous function: parameters, an arrow and a body, like &lt;code&gt;a -&amp;gt; a.weight() &amp;gt; 150&lt;/code&gt;. It can be passed wherever a functional interface is expected." data-book="Modern Java in Action">lambda</button> on several threads at once, and <code>acc.add</code> does <code>total += value</code> — a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Read-modify-write" data-def="A race in operations like &lt;code&gt;count++&lt;/code&gt;, which is really read, add one, write — another thread can update in between." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">read-modify-write</button> on a shared field.</p>
<p>Concurrent updates overwrite each other, so the total comes out wrong, and differently on each run.</p>
<p>Parallel streams must avoid shared mutable state; let the stream compute the result, as with <code>.sum()</code> or <code>.reduce(0L, Long::sum)</code>.</p>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">};</span></span></code></pre>80:T461,<p>javac moves the <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Lambda" data-def="An anonymous function: parameters, an arrow and a body, like &lt;code&gt;a -&amp;gt; a.weight() &amp;gt; 150&lt;/code&gt;. It can be passed wherever a functional interface is expected." data-book="Modern Java in Action">lambda</button> body into a private synthetic method, like <code>lambda$main$0</code>.</p>
<p>At the lambda expression, it emits an <code>invokedynamic</code> whose <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Bootstrap method" data-def="The method the JVM calls the first time an &lt;code&gt;invokedynamic&lt;/code&gt; instruction runs, to decide what that call site will do." data-book="The Well-Grounded Java Developer">bootstrap method</button> is <code>LambdaMetafactory.metafactory</code>.</p>
<p>The first time it runs, the factory spins up a class implementing <code>Runnable</code> that calls the synthetic method; later executions reuse the linked call site.</p>
<p>No class file per lambda, and the JDK can change the strategy without recompiling your code.</p>
81:T414,<p><code>-Xmx</code> limits only the heap. The JVM also needs memory for metaspace, thread stacks, the <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="JIT" data-def="The JVM&amp;#39;s just-in-time compiler — turns frequently run bytecode into optimized machine code while the program runs.">JIT</button>&#39;s code <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Cache" data-def="Keeping copies of data somewhere faster to reach, to avoid recomputing or refetching it.">cache</button>, <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Garbage collection" data-def="The JVM automatically finding objects that are no longer reachable and reclaiming their memory.">GC</button> data structures and direct buffers — often hundreds of megabytes.</p>
<p>With the heap allowed to fill the whole limit, the process eventually exceeds it, and the kernel&#39;s OOM killer terminates it with no Java stack trace.</p>
<p>Leaving headroom with <code>-XX:MaxRAMPercentage</code> avoids that.</p>
